Read the status with the finding. Findings include established vulnerabilities and poor practice to remediate or refactor. An exploit demonstration is not required for a poor-practice finding. Review means the evidence does not yet establish a problem or adequate protection. Protection applies only to the stated claim. No findings reported does not mean an entry is safe. Counts are per contextual use; shared helpers may appear more than once.

Confirmed vulnerability · static analysis

Path traversal supported by source analysis

A request-derived value reaches the path operand of fs.readFileSync (read) with no enforced containment (CWE-22): the value req.body reaches the path operand. This is a static source judgment under the declared posix assumption, not runtime exploitation, symbolic-link or filesystem-state analysis, or independent Findings Review.

Not established from the supplied evidence.

Protection at this use

Path assessment: traversal vulnerable, containment none, coverage incomplete. Scanner policy: A consistent traversal answer is supported by an independent source-to-operand path with a verified fs.readFileSync binding under the declared posix assumption; no enforced containment is established. Unresolved sibling paths remain visible. This is a static judgment, not runtime exploitation, symbolic-link or filesystem-state analysis, or independent Findings Review.

Fix scope & remaining questions

Review the evidence for the named claim. Independent findings review and runtime confirmation have not been performed. The platform assumption is posix; Windows path semantics are not modeled. Unresolved sibling paths, if any, remain separate blocking or coverage records.

Observed operation

fs.readFileSync(filePath, 'utf8')

pages/api/vulnerable-file-read.js:11

req.body

field_projection · pages/api/vulnerable-file-read.js:11

filePath

Contain this file operation

  • At pages/api/vulnerable-file-read.js:22, constrain the path built from req.body before fs.readFileSync(filePath, 'utf8'). Do not rely on path.join, path.resolve, os.path.join or normalization alone, a '..' text check, a try/except around the call, or an error response after the read.
  • Prefer selecting the file by a trusted identifier (a finite allow-list of known names or a stored record id) instead of accepting a path.
  • Otherwise resolve the final path and verify it stays inside the trusted directory at a path-component boundary (a separator-bounded prefix, a path.relative or os.path.commonpath check) before the file operation, rejecting on failure. A join, resolve or normalize call, a '..' blacklist and a prefix comparison without a separator are not containment.
  • If symbolic links inside the directory must not be followed, resolve with realpath before comparing and account for races between the check and the use.

Additional source context

pages/api/vulnerable-file-read.js:6

export default function handler(req, res) {
  if (req.method !== 'POST') {
    return res.status(405).json({ error: 'Method not allowed' });
  }

  const { filePath } = req.body;

  if (!filePath) {
    return res.status(400).json({ error: 'No file path provided' });
  }

  console.log(`[VULNERABLE] Reading file: ${filePath}`);

  try {
    // EXTREMELY DANGEROUS: Reading user-specified files without validation!
    // This allows attackers to read any file the server has access to
    const fileContent = fs.readFileSync(filePath, 'utf8');

    res.status(200).json({
      success: true,
      filePath: filePath,
      content: fileContent,
      size: fileContent.length,
      timestamp: Date.now()
    });
  } catch (error) {
    res.status(500).json({
      error: error.message,
      code: error.code,
      filePath: filePath
    });
  }
}

pages/api/vulnerable-file-read.js:6

export default function handler(req, res) {
  if (req.method !== 'POST') {
    return res.status(405).json({ error: 'Method not allowed' });
  }

  const { filePath } = req.body;

  if (!filePath) {
    return res.status(400).json({ error: 'No file path provided' });
  }

  console.log(`[VULNERABLE] Reading file: ${filePath}`);

  try {
    // EXTREMELY DANGEROUS: Reading user-specified files without validation!
    // This allows attackers to read any file the server has access to
    const fileContent = fs.readFileSync(filePath, 'utf8');

    res.status(200).json({
      success: true,
      filePath: filePath,
      content: fileContent,
      size: fileContent.length,
      timestamp: Date.now()
    });
  } catch (error) {
    res.status(500).json({
      error: error.message,
      code: error.code,
      filePath: filePath
    });
  }
}

pages/api/vulnerable-file-read.js:6

export default function handler(req, res) {
  if (req.method !== 'POST') {
    return res.status(405).json({ error: 'Method not allowed' });
  }

  const { filePath } = req.body;

  if (!filePath) {
    return res.status(400).json({ error: 'No file path provided' });
  }

  console.log(`[VULNERABLE] Reading file: ${filePath}`);

  try {
    // EXTREMELY DANGEROUS: Reading user-specified files without validation!
    // This allows attackers to read any file the server has access to
    const fileContent = fs.readFileSync(filePath, 'utf8');

    res.status(200).json({
      success: true,
      filePath: filePath,
      content: fileContent,
      size: fileContent.length,
      timestamp: Date.now()
    });
  } catch (error) {
    res.status(500).json({
      error: error.message,
      code: error.code,
      filePath: filePath
    });
  }
}

pages/api/vulnerable-exec.js:6

export default function handler(req, res) {
  if (req.method !== 'POST') {
    return res.status(405).json({ error: 'Method not allowed' });
  }

  const { command } = req.body;

  if (!command) {
    return res.status(400).json({ error: 'No command provided' });
  }

  console.log(`[VULNERABLE] Executing command: ${command}`);

  // EXTREMELY DANGEROUS: Executing user input directly!
  // This allows Remote Code Execution (RCE)
  exec(command, { timeout: 5000, maxBuffer: 1024 * 1024 }, (error, stdout, stderr) => {
    if (error) {
      console.error(`[VULNERABLE] Command error:`, error);
      return res.status(500).json({
        error: error.message,
        stderr: stderr,
        code: error.code,
        killed: error.killed
      });
    }

    console.log(`[VULNERABLE] Command output:`, stdout);

    res.status(200).json({
      success: true,
      command: command,
      output: stdout,
      stderr: stderr,
      timestamp: Date.now()
    });
  });
}

export default function handler(req, res) {
  if (req.method !== 'POST') {
    return res.status(405).json({ error: 'Method not allowed' });
  }

  const { command } = req.body;

  if (!command) {
    return res.status(400).json({ error: 'No command provided' });
  }

  console.log(`[VULNERABLE] Executing command: ${command}`);

  // EXTREMELY DANGEROUS: Executing user input directly!
  // This allows Remote Code Execution (RCE)
  exec(command, { timeout: 5000, maxBuffer: 1024 * 1024 }, (error, stdout, stderr) => {
    if (error) {
      console.error(`[VULNERABLE] Command error:`, error);
      return res.status(500).json({
        error: error.message,
        stderr: stderr,
        code: error.code,
        killed: error.killed
      });
    }

    console.log(`[VULNERABLE] Command output:`, stdout);

    res.status(200).json({
      success: true,
      command: command,
      output: stdout,
      stderr: stderr,
      timestamp: Date.now()
    });
  });
}

export default function handler(req, res) {
  if (req.method !== 'POST') {
    return res.status(405).json({ error: 'Method not allowed' });
  }

  const { command } = req.body;

  if (!command) {
    return res.status(400).json({ error: 'No command provided' });
  }

  console.log(`[VULNERABLE] Executing command: ${command}`);

  // EXTREMELY DANGEROUS: Executing user input directly!
  // This allows Remote Code Execution (RCE)
  exec(command, { timeout: 5000, maxBuffer: 1024 * 1024 }, (error, stdout, stderr) => {
    if (error) {

        error: error.message,
        stderr: stderr,
        code: error.code,
        killed: error.killed

    res.status(200).json({
      success: true,
      command: command,
      output: stdout,
      stderr: stderr,
      timestamp: Date.now()
    });
  });
}

pages/xss/unsafe/script-context.js:7

function ScriptContextUnsafe({ data }) {
  return <LabLayout title="XSS-003 · Script-context injection (SSR)">
    <p>Server-side rendering embeds this page's <code>data</code> prop into an inline <code>&lt;script&gt;</code> using <code>JSON.stringify</code>. JSON escaping handles quotes but not the HTML parser: the string <code>&lt;/script&gt;</code> terminates the script element no matter where it appears in the JSON, and everything after it is parsed as page markup.</p>
    <form method="GET">
      <label>data<input name="data" defaultValue={data} autoComplete="off" /></label>
      <div className="actions"><button>Reload with data</button><button type="button" onClick={() => { window.location.search = `?data=${encodeURIComponent(sample)}`; }}>Load breakout sample</button></div>
    </form>
    <h2>Embedded server data</h2>
    {/* XSS-003 / CWE-79: JSON embedded in a script context without escaping "</" for HTML. */}
    <script dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: `window.__labData = ${JSON.stringify({ data })};` }} />
    <p>Open the browser console and inspect <code>window.__labData</code> — then load the breakout sample and check <code>window.__zazzlesXss</code>.</p>
    <p>Safe comparison: <Link href="/xss/safe/script-context">the same JSON escaped for script embedding</Link>.</p>
  </LabLayout>;
}

export default endpoint(['POST'], async (req, res) => {
  const { username, password } = req.body || {};
  if (!validText(username, 100) || !validText(password, 200)) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Username and password must be nonempty strings (100/200 characters maximum).' });
  const db = await getDatabase();
  // SQL-001 / CWE-89: intentionally interpolate both attacker-controlled fields.
  const { rows } = await db.query(`SELECT id, username, role FROM users WHERE username = '${username}' AND password_demo = '${password}' LIMIT 1`);
  return res.status(rows.length ? 200 : 401).json({ authenticated: rows.length > 0, user: rows[0] || null });
}, { discloseErrors: true });

lib/http.cjs:1

function endpoint(methods, run, { discloseErrors = false } = {}) {
  return async function handler(req, res) {
    res.setHeader('Cache-Control', 'no-store');
    if (!methods.includes(req.method)) {
      res.setHeader('Allow', methods.join(', '));
      return res.status(405).json({ error: 'Method not allowed' });
    }
    try { return await run(req, res); }
    catch (error) {
      return res.status(500).json({ error: discloseErrors ? error.message : 'Request failed; reset the lab database if needed.' });
    }
  };
}

export default endpoint(['GET', 'POST'], async (req, res) => {
  const db = await getDatabase();
  if (req.method === 'GET') {
    const { rows } = await db.query('SELECT id, body, visibility FROM notes ORDER BY id');
    return res.status(200).json({ notes: rows });
  }
  const { body } = req.body || {};
  if (!validText(body)) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'body must be a nonempty string of at most 1000 characters.' });
  // SQL-003 / CWE-89: exec accepts multiple statements, enabling stacked SQL.
  const results = await db.exec(`INSERT INTO notes (body, visibility) VALUES ('${body}', 'public') RETURNING id, body, visibility`);
  return res.status(201).json({ notes: results[0].rows });
}, { discloseErrors: true });

lib/http.cjs:1

function endpoint(methods, run, { discloseErrors = false } = {}) {
  return async function handler(req, res) {
    res.setHeader('Cache-Control', 'no-store');
    if (!methods.includes(req.method)) {
      res.setHeader('Allow', methods.join(', '));
      return res.status(405).json({ error: 'Method not allowed' });
    }
    try { return await run(req, res); }
    catch (error) {
      return res.status(500).json({ error: discloseErrors ? error.message : 'Request failed; reset the lab database if needed.' });
    }
  };
}

pages/xss/unsafe/comments.js:6

function CommentsUnsafe({ comments }) {
  const [author, setAuthor] = useState('visitor');
  const [body, setBody] = useState('Hello from the lab');
  const [busy, setBusy] = useState(false);
  async function save(event) {
    event.preventDefault(); setBusy(true);
    try {
      await fetch('/api/xss/comments', { method: 'POST', headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' }, body: JSON.stringify({ author, body }) });
      window.location.reload();
    } finally { setBusy(false); }
  }
  return <LabLayout title="XSS-004 · Stored XSS in the guestbook (unsafe)">
    <p>A classic guestbook: comments are stored through a parameterized write (see <code>pages/api/xss/comments.js</code> — storage is not the flaw), and this page renders every stored comment as raw HTML. A payload saved once executes for <strong>every future visitor</strong>, which is what separates stored XSS from the reflected variant.</p>
    <form onSubmit={save}>
      <label>Author<input value={author} autoComplete="off" onChange={(e) => setAuthor(e.target.value)} /></label>
      <label>Comment<textarea rows={3} value={body} autoComplete="off" onChange={(e) => setBody(e.target.value)} /></label>
      <div className="actions"><button disabled={busy}>Post comment</button><button type="button" onClick={() => setBody(sample)}>Load XSS sample</button></div>
    </form>
    <h2>All comments</h2>
    {comments.map((comment) => (
      // XSS-004 / CWE-79: stored attacker-controlled strings rendered as raw HTML for every visitor.
      <div key={comment.id} className="xss-output note">
        <strong>{comment.author}</strong> <small>{comment.created_at}</small>
        <div className="comment-body" dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: comment.body }} />
      </div>
    ))}
    <p>Safe comparison: <Link href="/xss/sanitized/comments">the same comments rendered through DOMPurify</Link> · <Link href="/xss/safe/stored">plain text</Link>.</p>
  </LabLayout>;
}

lib/http.cjs:1

function endpoint(methods, run, { discloseErrors = false } = {}) {
  return async function handler(req, res) {
    res.setHeader('Cache-Control', 'no-store');
    if (!methods.includes(req.method)) {
      res.setHeader('Allow', methods.join(', '));
      return res.status(405).json({ error: 'Method not allowed' });
    }
    try { return await run(req, res); }
    catch (error) {
      return res.status(500).json({ error: discloseErrors ? error.message : 'Request failed; reset the lab database if needed.' });
    }
  };
}

function endpoint(methods, run, { discloseErrors = false } = {}) {
  return async function handler(req, res) {
    res.setHeader('Cache-Control', 'no-store');
    if (!methods.includes(req.method)) {
      res.setHeader('Allow', methods.join(', '));
      return res.status(405).json({ error: 'Method not allowed' });
    }
    try { return await run(req, res); }
    catch (error) {
      return res.status(500).json({ error: discloseErrors ? error.message : 'Request failed; reset the lab database if needed.' });
    }
  };
}

pages/api/protected-example.js:3

export default function handler(req, res) {
  if (req.method !== 'POST') {
    return res.status(405).json({ error: 'Method not allowed' });
  }

  const { name, message } = req.body;

  // Validate inputs
  if (!name || typeof name !== 'string') {
    return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Invalid name' });
  }

  if (!message || typeof message !== 'string') {
    return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Invalid message' });
  }

  // Sanitize inputs - only allow alphanumeric and basic punctuation
  const sanitizedName = name.replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9\s\-_.]/g, '');
  const sanitizedMessage = message.replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9\s\-_.!?,]/g, '');

  // Validate length
  if (sanitizedName.length > 100) {

pages/api/protected-example.js:3

export default function handler(req, res) {
  if (req.method !== 'POST') {
    return res.status(405).json({ error: 'Method not allowed' });
  }

  const { name, message } = req.body;

  // Validate inputs
  if (!name || typeof name !== 'string') {
    return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Invalid name' });
  }

  if (!message || typeof message !== 'string') {
    return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Invalid message' });
  }

  // Sanitize inputs - only allow alphanumeric and basic punctuation
  const sanitizedName = name.replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9\s\-_.]/g, '');
  const sanitizedMessage = message.replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9\s\-_.!?,]/g, '');

  // Validate length
  if (sanitizedName.length > 100) {
    return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Name too long' });
  }

  if (sanitizedMessage.length > 1000) {
    return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Message too long' });
  }

  // Safe processing - no eval, no exec
  const result = {
    name: sanitizedName,
    message: sanitizedMessage,
    timestamp: Date.now(),
    processedBy: 'protected API'
  };

  res.status(200).json(result);
}

export default endpoint(['POST'], (req, res) => {
  const { text } = req.body || {};
  if (!validText(text, 200)) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'text must be a nonempty string of at most 200 characters.' });
  execFile('/bin/echo', [text], { shell: false, timeout: 5000, maxBuffer: 64 * 1024 }, (error, stdout, stderr) => {
    if (error) return res.status(500).json({ error: 'Echo failed.', stderr });
    return res.status(200).json({ echoed: stdout.trimEnd() });
  });
}, { discloseErrors: false });

lib/http.cjs:1

function endpoint(methods, run, { discloseErrors = false } = {}) {
  return async function handler(req, res) {
    res.setHeader('Cache-Control', 'no-store');
    if (!methods.includes(req.method)) {
      res.setHeader('Allow', methods.join(', '));
      return res.status(405).json({ error: 'Method not allowed' });
    }
    try { return await run(req, res); }
    catch (error) {
      return res.status(500).json({ error: discloseErrors ? error.message : 'Request failed; reset the lab database if needed.' });
    }
  };
}

pages/api/sql/zod/notes.js:11

export default endpoint(['GET', 'POST'], async (req, res) => {
  const db = await getDatabase();
  if (req.method === 'GET') {
    const { rows } = await db.query('SELECT id, body, visibility FROM notes ORDER BY id');
    return res.status(200).json({ notes: rows });
  }
  const parsed = Note.safeParse(req.body ?? {});
  if (!parsed.success) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Invalid note payload.', issues: parsed.error.issues.length });
  const result = await db.query('INSERT INTO notes (body, visibility) VALUES ($1, $2) RETURNING id, body, visibility', [parsed.data.body, 'public']);
  return res.status(201).json({ notes: result.rows, validatedWith: 'zod' });
}, { discloseErrors: false });

lib/http.cjs:1

function endpoint(methods, run, { discloseErrors = false } = {}) {
  return async function handler(req, res) {
    res.setHeader('Cache-Control', 'no-store');
    if (!methods.includes(req.method)) {
      res.setHeader('Allow', methods.join(', '));
      return res.status(405).json({ error: 'Method not allowed' });
    }
    try { return await run(req, res); }
    catch (error) {
      return res.status(500).json({ error: discloseErrors ? error.message : 'Request failed; reset the lab database if needed.' });
    }
  };
}

    catch (error) {

    }
  };

function getDatabase() {
  if (!globalThis.__zazzlesLabDatabase) {
    globalThis.__zazzlesLabDatabase = createDatabase().catch((error) => {
      delete globalThis.__zazzlesLabDatabase;
      throw error;
    });
  }
  return globalThis.__zazzlesLabDatabase;
}

async function createDatabase() {
  const db = await PGlite.create(); // No path: no persistence, no database service.

  catch (error) { await db.close(); throw error; }
}

pages/api/vulnerable-submit.js:4

export default function handler(req, res) {
  if (req.method !== 'POST') {
    return res.status(405).json({ error: 'Method not allowed' });
  }

  const { name, message } = req.body;

  try {
    // EXTREMELY DANGEROUS: Using eval with user input
    // This is the core vulnerability - attackers can break out of the string
    // and execute arbitrary JavaScript code
    const result = eval(`({
      name: '${name}',
      message: '${message}',
      timestamp: Date.now(),
      processedBy: 'vulnerable-submit API'
    })`);

    res.status(200).json(result);
  } catch (error) {
    res.status(500).json({
      error: error.message,
      stack: error.stack
    });
  }
}

pages/api/vulnerable-submit.js:4

export default function handler(req, res) {
  if (req.method !== 'POST') {
    return res.status(405).json({ error: 'Method not allowed' });
  }

  const { name, message } = req.body;

  try {
    // EXTREMELY DANGEROUS: Using eval with user input
    // This is the core vulnerability - attackers can break out of the string
    // and execute arbitrary JavaScript code
    const result = eval(`({
      name: '${name}',
      message: '${message}',
      timestamp: Date.now(),
      processedBy: 'vulnerable-submit API'
    })`);

    res.status(200).json(result);
  } catch (error) {
    res.status(500).json({
      error: error.message,
      stack: error.stack
    });
  }
}

pages/xss/safe/script-context.js:6

function jsonForScriptEmbedding(value) {
  return JSON.stringify(value).replace(/</g, '\\u003c').replace(/\u2028|\u2029/g, '\\u2028');
}

function ReflectedSanitized({ payload, clean }) {
  const [draft, setDraft] = useState(payload);
  return <LabLayout title="Safe control · DOMPurify-sanitized HTML">
    <p>Library comparison for <Link href="/xss/unsafe/reflected">XSS-001</Link>: when a page genuinely must render untrusted markup, sanitize it with <a href="https://github.com/cure53/DOMPurify">DOMPurify</a> before it reaches <code>dangerouslySetInnerHTML</code>. Event handlers and scripts are stripped; benign tags survive. Note for SAST ground truth: the raw sink is still present — it is safe by construction (sanitizer directly upstream), which many tools report differently from the unsanitized sink.</p>
    <form method="GET">
      <label>Payload<textarea name="payload" rows={4} value={draft} autoComplete="off" onChange={(e) => setDraft(e.target.value)} /></label>
      <div className="actions"><button>Render payload</button><button type="button" onClick={() => setDraft(sample)}>Load XSS sample</button><button type="button" onClick={() => setDraft('<b>bold</b> and <i>italic</i> markup')}>Load benign markup</button></div>
    </form>

export function getServerSideProps({ query }) {

function ReflectedSanitized({ payload, clean }) {
  const [draft, setDraft] = useState(payload);
  return <LabLayout title="Safe control · DOMPurify-sanitized HTML">
    <p>Library comparison for <Link href="/xss/unsafe/reflected">XSS-001</Link>: when a page genuinely must render untrusted markup, sanitize it with <a href="https://github.com/cure53/DOMPurify">DOMPurify</a> before it reaches <code>dangerouslySetInnerHTML</code>. Event handlers and scripts are stripped; benign tags survive. Note for SAST ground truth: the raw sink is still present — it is safe by construction (sanitizer directly upstream), which many tools report differently from the unsanitized sink.</p>
    <form method="GET">
      <label>Payload<textarea name="payload" rows={4} value={draft} autoComplete="off" onChange={(e) => setDraft(e.target.value)} /></label>
      <div className="actions"><button>Render payload</button><button type="button" onClick={() => setDraft(sample)}>Load XSS sample</button><button type="button" onClick={() => setDraft('<b>bold</b> and <i>italic</i> markup')}>Load benign markup</button></div>
    </form>
    <h2>Rendered output</h2>
    {/* XSS-001 library control: DOMPurify.sanitize() applied before raw rendering. */}
    <div id="xss-output" className="xss-output" dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: clean }} />
    <p>Text comparison: <Link href="/xss/safe/reflected">plain React text rendering</Link> needs no sanitizer at all.</p>
  </LabLayout>;
}

pages/api/sql/zod/login.js:14

export default endpoint(['POST'], async (req, res) => {
  const parsed = Credentials.safeParse(req.body ?? {});
  if (!parsed.success) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Invalid credentials payload.', issues: parsed.error.issues.length });
  const { username, password } = parsed.data;
  const db = await getDatabase();
  const { rows } = await db.query('SELECT id, username, role, password_hash FROM users WHERE username = $1', [username]);
  const user = rows[0];
  if (!user || !verifyPassword(password, user.password_hash)) return res.status(401).json({ authenticated: false });
  const { password_hash, ...identity } = user;
  return res.status(200).json({ authenticated: true, user: identity, validatedWith: 'zod' });
}, { discloseErrors: false });

lib/http.cjs:1

function endpoint(methods, run, { discloseErrors = false } = {}) {
  return async function handler(req, res) {
    res.setHeader('Cache-Control', 'no-store');
    if (!methods.includes(req.method)) {
      res.setHeader('Allow', methods.join(', '));
      return res.status(405).json({ error: 'Method not allowed' });
    }
    try { return await run(req, res); }
    catch (error) {
      return res.status(500).json({ error: discloseErrors ? error.message : 'Request failed; reset the lab database if needed.' });
    }
  };
}

function getDatabase() {

lib/http.cjs:1

function endpoint(methods, run, { discloseErrors = false } = {}) {
  return async function handler(req, res) {
    res.setHeader('Cache-Control', 'no-store');
    if (!methods.includes(req.method)) {
      res.setHeader('Allow', methods.join(', '));
      return res.status(405).json({ error: 'Method not allowed' });
    }
    try { return await run(req, res); }
    catch (error) {
      return res.status(500).json({ error: discloseErrors ? error.message : 'Request failed; reset the lab database if needed.' });
    }
  };
}

lib/database.cjs:58

function getDatabase() {
  if (!globalThis.__zazzlesLabDatabase) {
    globalThis.__zazzlesLabDatabase = createDatabase().catch((error) => {
      delete globalThis.__zazzlesLabDatabase;
      throw error;
    });
  }
  return globalThis.__zazzlesLabDatabase;
}

lib/database.cjs:50

async function createDatabase() {
  const db = await PGlite.create(); // No path: no persistence, no database service.
  try { await seed(db); return db; }
  catch (error) { await db.close(); throw error; }
}

pages/secure/csp-enabled.js:138

export async function getServerSideProps({ query, res }) {
  // Set CSP headers for this specific page
  res.setHeader(
    'Content-Security-Policy',
    "default-src 'self'; script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'; img-src 'self' data: https:; font-src 'self'; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'self'; form-action 'self'; frame-ancestors 'none'"
  );
  res.setHeader('X-Frame-Options', 'DENY');
  res.setHeader('X-Content-Type-Options', 'nosniff');
  res.setHeader('X-XSS-Protection', '1; mode=block');

  const userInput = query.input || 'No input provided';

  // Sanitize even though we have CSP
  const sanitized = typeof userInput === 'string'
    ? userInput.replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9\s\-_.]/g, '')
    : 'Invalid input';

  return {
    props: {
      userInput: sanitized
    }
  };
}

pages/secure/csp-enabled.js:3

function CSPEnabledPage({ userInput }) {
  return (
    <div style={{ padding: '2rem', maxWidth: '1200px', margin: '0 auto' }}>
      <Link href="/">← Back to Home</Link>

      <h1 style={{ color: '#00ff00' }}>✅ Secure: Content Security Policy Enabled</h1>

      <div style={{ background: '#00ff00', color: '#000', padding: '1rem', margin: '1rem 0', borderRadius: '5px' }}>
        ✅ SECURE: This page has Content Security Policy headers enabled!
      </div>

      <h2>What is CSP?</h2>
      <p>
        Content Security Policy (CSP) is an added layer of security that helps detect and mitigate
        certain types of attacks, including Cross-Site Scripting (XSS) and data injection attacks.
      </p>

      <h3>CSP Configuration:</h3>
      <pre>{`// next.config.js
const securityHeaders = [
  {
    key: 'Content-Security-Policy',
    value: [
      "default-src 'self'",
      "script-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'",
      "style-src 'self' 'unsafe-inline'",
      "img-src 'self' data: https:",
      "font-src 'self'",
      "object-src 'none'",
      "base-uri 'self'",
      "form-action 'self'",
      "frame-ancestors 'none'",
      "upgrade-insecure-requests"
    ].join('; ')
  },
  {
    key: 'X-Frame-Options',
    value: 'DENY'
  },
  {
    key: 'X-Content-Type-Options',
    value: 'nosniff'
  },
  {
    key: 'X-XSS-Protection',
    value: '1; mode=block'
  },
  {
    key: 'Referrer-Policy',
    value: 'strict-origin-when-cross-origin'
  }
];

module.exports = {
  async headers() {
    return [
      {
        source: '/:path*',
        headers: securityHeaders,
      },
    ];
  },
};`}</pre>

      <h3>Security Headers Applied:</h3>
      <div style={{ background: '#1a1a1a', padding: '1.5rem', borderRadius: '5px', margin: '1rem 0' }}>
        <ul>
          <li>✅ <strong>Content-Security-Policy:</strong> Restricts resource loading</li>
          <li>✅ <strong>X-Frame-Options:</strong> Prevents clickjacking</li>
          <li>✅ <strong>X-Content-Type-Options:</strong> Prevents MIME sniffing</li>
          <li>✅ <strong>X-XSS-Protection:</strong> Enables XSS filter</li>
          <li>Referrer-Policy appears in the sample configuration; this page does not set it.</li>
        </ul>
      </div>

      <h3>Test CSP Protection:</h3>
      <p>Open your browser's developer console. This page sets a CSP, but unsafe-inline still permits inline scripts. It is a limited teaching example, not a strict production policy.</p>

import { NextResponse } from 'next/server';

export function middleware(request) {
  const url = request.nextUrl.clone();

  // Check for suspicious patterns
  const suspiciousPatterns = [
    /eval\\(/,
    /require\\(/,
    /child_process/,
    /fs\\./,
    /__dirname/,
    /__filename/
  ];

  const queryString = url.searchParams.toString();

  for (const pattern of suspiciousPatterns) {
    if (pattern.test(queryString)) {
      return new NextResponse('Blocked', { status: 403 });
    }
  }

  return NextResponse.next();
}`}</pre>

pages/secure/redirect.js:6

export function getServerSideProps({ query }) {
  const to = typeof query.to === 'string' ? query.to : '';
  // Must start with "/" but not "//" (protocol-relative) and contain no scheme.
  const allowed = /^\/[^/]/.test(to) && !/[a-z][a-z0-9+.-]*:/i.test(to) && to.length <= 2000;
  if (allowed) return { redirect: { destination: to, permanent: false } };
  return { props: { to } };
}

pages/secure/sanitized-input.js:127

export async function getServerSideProps({ query }) {
  const userInput = query.input || 'Hello World';

  // Step 1: Type validation
  if (typeof userInput !== 'string') {
    return {
      props: {
        userInput: '',
        sanitizedInput: 'Invalid input type',
        rawQuery: 'Type validation failed'
      }
    };
  }

  // Step 2: Length validation
  if (userInput.length > 200) {
    return {
      props: {
        userInput: '',
        sanitizedInput: 'Input too long',
        rawQuery: userInput.substring(0, 50) + '...'
      }
    };
  }

  // Step 3: Sanitization - only allow safe characters
  const sanitizedInput = userInput.replace(/[^a-zA-Z0-9\s\-_.]/g, '');

  // Step 4: Additional validation - check for dangerous patterns
  const dangerousPatterns = [
    /eval\(/i,
    /require\(/i,
    /child_process/i,
    /exec\(/i,
    /<script/i,
    /javascript:/i,
    /\$\{/,  // Template literals
    /process\./i
  ];

  for (const pattern of dangerousPatterns) {
    if (pattern.test(userInput)) {
      return {
        props: {
          userInput: '',
          sanitizedInput: 'Blocked: Dangerous pattern detected',
          rawQuery: 'Security check failed'
        }
      };
    }
  }

  return {
    props: {
      userInput,
      sanitizedInput,
      rawQuery: userInput
    }
  };
}

export async function getServerSideProps({ req, query }) {
  const target = typeof query.fetch === 'string' && query.fetch.length <= 2000 ? query.fetch : '/api/sql/safe/products';
  try {
    // Resolve against this request's own origin, then enforce host + path prefix.
    const url = new URL(target, `http://${req.headers.host ?? '127.0.0.1:3000'}`);
    if (url.origin !== `http://${req.headers.host}`) return { props: { url: target, blocked: 'target resolves to a different origin' } };
    if (!url.pathname.startsWith('/api/')) return { props: { url: target, blocked: 'only /api/ paths are fetchable' } };
    const response = await fetch(url, { redirect: 'manual', signal: AbortSignal.timeout(5000) });
    const excerpt = (await response.text()).slice(0, 500);
    return { props: { url: target, status: response.status, contentType: response.headers.get('content-type') ?? '', excerpt } };
  } catch (error) {
    return { props: { url: target, error: error.message } };
  }
}

pages/sql/[operation].js:9

function SqlLab({ operation }) {
  const example = examples[operation];
  const [mode, setMode] = useState('unsafe');
  const [values, setValues] = useState(example.defaults);
  const [result, setResult] = useState(null);
  const [busy, setBusy] = useState(false);
  async function request(event) {
    event.preventDefault(); setBusy(true);
    try {
      const url = `/api/sql/${mode}/${operation}`;
      const response = await fetch(operation === 'products' ? `${url}?${new URLSearchParams(values)}` : url,
        operation === 'products' ? {} : { method: 'POST', headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' }, body: JSON.stringify(values) });
      setResult({ status: response.status, body: await response.json() });
    } catch (error) { setResult({ error: error.message }); }
    finally { setBusy(false); }
  }
  async function reset() {
    setBusy(true);
    try {
      const response = await fetch('/api/lab/reset', { method: 'POST' });
      setResult({ status: response.status, body: await response.json() });
    } catch (error) { setResult({ error: error.message }); }
    finally { setBusy(false); }
  }
  return <LabLayout title={`${example.id} · ${example.title}`}>
    <nav className="tabs">{Object.keys(examples).map((key) => <Link key={key} href={`/sql/${key}`}>{examples[key].title}</Link>)}</nav>
    <p>Compare the same input against unsafe SQL interpolation and safe parameterized queries. Data is synthetic and held in server memory.</p>
    {operation === 'login' && <p>This checks a demo identity; it creates no session. Credentials: alice / alice-lab-only or admin / admin-lab-only.</p>}
    {operation === 'products' && <p>Two public products should be visible. The unsafe payload also reveals Internal prototype.</p>}
    {operation === 'notes' && <p>The unsafe sample makes the private product public through a second SQL statement. Check product search afterward, then reset the database.</p>}
    <form onSubmit={request}><label>Query mode<select value={mode} onChange={(e) => setMode(e.target.value)}><option value="unsafe">Unsafe · intentional SQL injection</option><option value="safe">Safe · parameterized SQL</option><option value="zod">Safe · Zod schema validation + parameters</option></select></label>
      {Object.entries(values).map(([key, value]) => <label key={key}>{key}<input value={value} autoComplete="off" onChange={(e) => setValues({ ...values, [key]: e.target.value })} /></label>)}
      <div className="actions"><button disabled={busy}>Run {mode} example</button><button type="button" onClick={() => setValues(example.payload)}>Load injection example</button><button type="button" onClick={() => setValues(example.defaults)}>Normal input</button><button type="button" disabled={busy} onClick={reset}>Reset SQL data</button></div>
    </form><p>Endpoint: <code>/api/sql/{mode}/{operation}</code></p>{result && <pre aria-live="polite">{JSON.stringify(result, null, 2)}</pre>}
  </LabLayout>;
}

pages/vulnerable/eval-injection.js:3

function EvalInjection({ userInput }) {
  const [result, setResult] = useState('');
  useEffect(() => {
    try {
      // JS-001 / CWE-95: intentional browser-side code injection.
      eval(`setResult('${userInput}')`);
    } catch (error) { setResult(error.message); }
  }, [userInput]);
  return <LabLayout title="JS-001 · Browser eval injection"><p>The input query parameter enters eval inside useEffect. This is browser JavaScript execution / XSS; it does not execute in server-side rendering.</p><p>Harmless marker: append <code>{"?input=' + (6 * 7) + '"}</code> to the URL. Expected result: 42.</p><h2>Result</h2><pre>{result}</pre><p>Safe comparison: <a href="/secure/sanitized-input">sanitized input</a>.</p></LabLayout>;
}

export function getServerSideProps({ query }) {

pages/xss/sanitized/comments.js:7

function CommentsSanitized({ comments }) {
  const [author, setAuthor] = useState('visitor');
  const [body, setBody] = useState('Hello from the lab');
  const [busy, setBusy] = useState(false);
  async function save(event) {
    event.preventDefault(); setBusy(true);
    try {
      await fetch('/api/xss/comments', { method: 'POST', headers: { 'Content-Type': 'application/json' }, body: JSON.stringify({ author, body }) });
      window.location.reload();
    } finally { setBusy(false); }
  }
  return <LabLayout title="Safe control · Sanitized guestbook rendering">
    <p>Protected comparison for <Link href="/xss/unsafe/comments">XSS-004</Link>: identical storage, identical list — but every stored body is passed through <a href="https://github.com/cure53/DOMPurify">DOMPurify</a> during server-side rendering before it reaches <code>dangerouslySetInnerHTML</code>. Benign markup (like the seeded <code>&lt;b&gt;</code> tag) survives; event handlers and scripts are stripped. This is the pattern for pages that must render rich text.</p>
    <form onSubmit={save}>
      <label>Author<input value={author} autoComplete="off" onChange={(e) => setAuthor(e.target.value)} /></label>
      <label>Comment<textarea rows={3} value={body} autoComplete="off" onChange={(e) => setBody(e.target.value)} /></label>
      <div className="actions"><button disabled={busy}>Post comment</button><button type="button" onClick={() => setBody(sample)}>Load XSS sample</button><button type="button" onClick={() => setBody('Trying out <b>bold</b> and <i>italic</i>')}>Load benign markup</button></div>
    </form>
    <h2>All comments</h2>
    {comments.map((comment) => (
      <div key={comment.id} className="xss-output note">
        <strong>{comment.author}</strong> <small>{comment.created_at}</small>
        {/* XSS-004 library control: DOMPurify.sanitize() upstream of the raw sink. */}
        <div className="comment-body" dangerouslySetInnerHTML={{ __html: DOMPurify.sanitize(comment.body) }} />
      </div>
    ))}
    <p>The seeded <code>&lt;b&gt;benign markup&lt;/b&gt;</code> comment still renders bold; the XSS sample's handler is gone.</p>
  </LabLayout>;
}

export default endpoint(['POST'], async (req, res) => {
  const { username, password } = req.body || {};
  if (!validText(username, 100) || !validText(password, 200)) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'Username and password must be nonempty strings (100/200 characters maximum).' });
  const db = await getDatabase();
  const { rows } = await db.query('SELECT id, username, role, password_hash FROM users WHERE username = $1', [username]);
  const user = rows[0];
  if (!user || !verifyPassword(password, user.password_hash)) return res.status(401).json({ authenticated: false });
  const { password_hash, ...identity } = user;
  return res.status(200).json({ authenticated: true, user: identity });
}, { discloseErrors: false });

lib/http.cjs:1

function endpoint(methods, run, { discloseErrors = false } = {}) {
  return async function handler(req, res) {
    res.setHeader('Cache-Control', 'no-store');
    if (!methods.includes(req.method)) {
      res.setHeader('Allow', methods.join(', '));
      return res.status(405).json({ error: 'Method not allowed' });
    }
    try { return await run(req, res); }
    catch (error) {
      return res.status(500).json({ error: discloseErrors ? error.message : 'Request failed; reset the lab database if needed.' });
    }
  };
}

function getDatabase() {
  if (!globalThis.__zazzlesLabDatabase) {
    globalThis.__zazzlesLabDatabase = createDatabase().catch((error) => {
      delete globalThis.__zazzlesLabDatabase;
      throw error;
    });
  }
  return globalThis.__zazzlesLabDatabase;
}

  catch (error) { await db.close(); throw error; }
}

export default endpoint(['GET', 'POST'], async (req, res) => {
  const db = await getDatabase();
  if (req.method === 'GET') {
    const { rows } = await db.query('SELECT id, body, visibility FROM notes ORDER BY id');
    return res.status(200).json({ notes: rows });
  }
  const { body } = req.body || {};
  if (!validText(body)) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'body must be a nonempty string of at most 1000 characters.' });
  const result = await db.query('INSERT INTO notes (body, visibility) VALUES ($1, $2) RETURNING id, body, visibility', [body, 'public']);
  return res.status(201).json({ notes: result.rows });
}, { discloseErrors: false });

  if (!validText(body)) return res.status(400).json({ error: 'body must be a nonempty string of at most 1000 characters.' });
  const result = await db.query('INSERT INTO notes (body, visibility) VALUES ($1, $2) RETURNING id, body, visibility', [body, 'public']);
  return res.status(201).json({ notes: result.rows });
}

lib/http.cjs:1

function endpoint(methods, run, { discloseErrors = false } = {}) {
  return async function handler(req, res) {
    res.setHeader('Cache-Control', 'no-store');
    if (!methods.includes(req.method)) {
      res.setHeader('Allow', methods.join(', '));
      return res.status(405).json({ error: 'Method not allowed' });
    }
    try { return await run(req, res); }
    catch (error) {
      return res.status(500).json({ error: discloseErrors ? error.message : 'Request failed; reset the lab database if needed.' });
    }
  };
}

  try { await seed(db); return db; }
  catch (error) { await db.close(); throw error; }
}

Scope & limitations

  • Verified Express receiver construction and direct registrations required; chained routes, mount composition and effective middleware are not resolved
  • Supported helpers bind identifier parameters and direct returns; finite literal tables and local literal-argument factories retain closure contexts. Unsupported branches/captures, wrappers, unknown calls, recursion and asynchronous continuations remain gaps
  • SQL text, NoSQL $where strings, query documents, and request-field validation are candidate findings, not confirmed vulnerabilities

Finding-type glossary · 14 definitions

Types identify security review areas. A type name does not establish vulnerability or detector coverage; always read the result status.

Client-side XSS

Coverage: bounded browser DOM and Angular/React/Vue analysis

Browser or API data reaches a DOM or framework rendering operation in an active output context.

How to interpret this type

Client entries identify browser sources and verified sinks. Raw flows are construction findings; unknown origin, trust-to-template linkage or API response mapping remains Review. Framework defaults can protect a specific output.

Remediation approach

Prefer text APIs and default framework escaping/sanitization. Sanitize rich HTML before rendering, remove unnecessary trust bypasses and verify API origins.

Rendering Flow Review

Coverage: bounded API response evidence

Data returned by an API needs its browser consumer linked before a rendering claim can be assessed.

How to interpret this type

JSON output alone is not an XSS sink or a vulnerability. This record is incomplete flow context for further analysis.

Remediation approach

Locate the browser consumer, establish its output context and protections, and assess that rendering flow before changing API data handling.

SQL Injection

Coverage: bounded analysis

Input changes executable SQL structure instead of remaining a data value, potentially changing what the database reads or writes.

How to interpret this type

An SQL Injection pill identifies the risk family being reviewed. String-built executable SQL is poor practice to remediate even without a proven exploit; a library-managed search value may remain unresolved. The finding status and evidence determine the actual claim. The pill alone does not confirm injection.

Remediation approach

Use the verified driver's parameter-binding API for values. Review dynamic identifiers separately. Keep business-field validation independent of query-structure protection.

NoSQL Injection

Coverage: bounded analysis

Untrusted input changes a NoSQL query's operators, selector structure, or executable expression rather than remaining an allowed data value.

How to interpret this type

Executable $where string construction is poor practice to refactor even without a proven exploit. An unmodeled document constraint alone is Review, not an established defect. Inspect actual value types, keys, transformations, library behavior, and the named status.

Remediation approach

Prefer data-only query APIs, constrain scalar slots and permitted keys/operators, and avoid building executable $where strings from input.

Command Injection

Coverage: bounded Node child_process and Python os/subprocess analysis

Request-controlled values reach an operating-system process: shell command text, the executable, a separate argument or process shell configuration.

How to interpret this type

One record per process use, with separate shell-syntax, executable-selection and argument facets under a declared POSIX assumption. A confirmed record is a static source judgment, not runtime exploitation or independent Findings Review. Shell-disabled argument arrays do not by themselves establish protection; an unknown utility's options and unmodeled platform behavior remain Review.

Remediation approach

Prefer eliminating process execution. Otherwise use a fixed trusted absolute executable, pass values as separate arguments with the shell disabled, and enforce utility-specific constraints such as a finite set of permitted values. Do not rely on stripping punctuation or on `--` as a universal option terminator.

Path Traversal

Coverage: bounded Node fs and Python open/io.open/pathlib read and write analysis

A request-controlled value selects the file a read or write operation opens, so '..' segments, an absolute path or an unconstrained name can reach files outside the intended directory.

How to interpret this type

One record per filesystem use, with separate traversal, containment-control and coverage assessments under a declared POSIX assumption. A confirmed record is a static source judgment (CWE-22), not runtime exploitation or independent Findings Review. Normalization, join or resolve calls, a '..' blacklist, a prefix check without a separator and error handling do not establish containment. Framework file-serving wrappers, uploads, deletes, archives and symbolic-link or race behavior are not modeled and stay Review or uncovered.

Remediation approach

Select files by a trusted identifier or a finite allow-list. Otherwise resolve the final path and enforce a path-component-boundary containment check against the trusted directory before the operation, and decide deliberately how symbolic links are treated.

Input Validation

Coverage: bounded analysis

Server-side checks enforce the business requirements for a field, such as required presence, runtime type, permitted format, range, or length.

How to interpret this type

Poor input validation means an applicable requirement is not adequately enforced. This neutral family label also covers unresolved checks and supported protection: no validator identified in an analyzed slice is not proof that every execution path lacks validation. Free-text punctuation is not itself a defect.

Remediation approach

Define field-specific requirements and enforce them before use on all applicable paths. Verify guard behavior and middleware. Apply destination-specific protection separately.

Account Creation Validation

Coverage: bounded analysis

Required account fields and enforcing guards must be checked before an account is persisted.

How to interpret this type

Review field requirements, conditional bypasses, and generated persistence behavior. A modeled create operation alone does not prove a registration bypass.

Remediation approach

Enforce required fields before persistence, terminate rejected paths, add appropriate database constraints, and test the persisted result.

Server-Side Request Forgery

Coverage: bounded global fetch, axios and Python requests/httpx/urllib destination analysis

A request-controlled value chooses the destination of an outbound HTTP request the server makes, so it can reach hosts the requester cannot reach directly.

How to interpret this type

One record per outbound call whose destination operand a request value reaches through a verified client, with separate destination-control, restriction and coverage assessments. A confirmed record is a static source judgment (CWE-918), not network reachability, DNS, redirect or deployment-policy analysis. A string prefix test is poor practice, not a restriction. Second-order destinations and unverified clients stay outbound observations.

Remediation approach

Select destinations from a fixed table of trusted URLs. Otherwise parse the URL, allow-list scheme and host before the request, and disable or re-check redirects.

Outbound Request Safety

Coverage: bounded analysis

Input used in an outbound request may affect its destination, headers, or other request values. Unrestricted destination control can lead to server-side request forgery (SSRF).

How to interpret this type

The current scanner records bounded outbound-use evidence. A pill does not establish SSRF, destination reachability, or complete response-flow analysis.

Remediation approach

Verify allowed destinations and protocols, redirect handling, network boundaries, and sensitive request values for the specific HTTP client.

Reflected XSS

Coverage: bounded server response and template analysis

Request-controlled content is reflected into a browser response in a context that allows it to execute as script.

How to interpret this type

Request input reaches an HTML response or template output. Raw output is a construction finding; unknown context or sanitizers remain Review. Escaping protects only its modeled output context. Runtime exploitation is not implied.

Remediation approach

Use context-appropriate output encoding and safe template or DOM APIs. Input validation alone does not replace correct output handling.

Stored XSS

Coverage: bounded model-field write/read and server template analysis

Untrusted data is persisted and later rendered in an active browser context.

How to interpret this type

A matching model and field link source writes to rendering reads. Unresolved write origins remain Review; a verified raw path is a construction finding. A dependency version alone does not establish a reachable XSS flow.

Remediation approach

Preserve the intended stored data; apply context-appropriate escaping or maintained sanitization at every rendering sink, and review existing stored content.

Sensitive Data Logging

Coverage: bounded analysis

Logs expose credentials, tokens, personal information, or other sensitive values to readers or storage that should not receive them.

How to interpret this type

Covers verified console and winston calls in analyzed route handlers that receive request values with credential or personal-data evidence. A field name is a hint for review, not proof; Authorization and Cookie headers are credentials by HTTP semantics. Morgan request logging that records URLs is checked against credentials read from the query string. Constant messages are not leaks. Non-request secrets and logger redaction configuration are not yet analyzed.

Remediation approach

Avoid logging secrets; minimize sensitive fields and apply verified redaction before emission. Review log access and retention.

Sensitive Operation Review

Coverage: bounded analysis

A possible sensitive use was recorded but does not yet map to a more specific supported risk family.

How to interpret this type

This is an analysis category. Inspect the evidence and missing operation semantics before making a security claim.

Remediation approach

Resolve the source, operation, API behavior, and required protection before selecting a fix.

A confirmed record is a static source judgment (CWE-22), not runtime exploitation or independent Findings Review. Normalization, join or resolve calls, a '..' blacklist, a prefix check without a separator and error handling do not establish containment. Framework file-serving wrappers, uploads, deletes, archives and symbolic-link or race behavior are not modeled and stay Review or uncovered.

Remediation approach

Select files by a trusted identifier or a finite allow-list. Otherwise resolve the final path and enforce a path-component-boundary containment check against the trusted directory before the operation, and decide deliberately how symbolic links are treated.

Finding-type glossary

Input Validation

Coverage: bounded analysis

Server-side checks enforce the business requirements for a field, such as required presence, runtime type, permitted format, range, or length.

What this label tells you

Poor input validation means an applicable requirement is not adequately enforced. This neutral family label also covers unresolved checks and supported protection: no validator identified in an analyzed slice is not proof that every execution path lacks validation. Free-text punctuation is not itself a defect.

Remediation approach

Define field-specific requirements and enforce them before use on all applicable paths. Verify guard behavior and middleware. Apply destination-specific protection separately.

Finding-type glossary

Account Creation Validation

Coverage: bounded analysis

Required account fields and enforcing guards must be checked before an account is persisted.

What this label tells you

Review field requirements, conditional bypasses, and generated persistence behavior. A modeled create operation alone does not prove a registration bypass.

Remediation approach

Enforce required fields before persistence, terminate rejected paths, add appropriate database constraints, and test the persisted result.

Finding-type glossary

Server-Side Request Forgery

Coverage: bounded global fetch, axios and Python requests/httpx/urllib destination analysis

A request-controlled value chooses the destination of an outbound HTTP request the server makes, so it can reach hosts the requester cannot reach directly.

What this label tells you

One record per outbound call whose destination operand a request value reaches through a verified client, with separate destination-control, restriction and coverage assessments. A confirmed record is a static source judgment (CWE-918), not network reachability, DNS, redirect or deployment-policy analysis. A string prefix test is poor practice, not a restriction. Second-order destinations and unverified clients stay outbound observations.

Remediation approach

Select destinations from a fixed table of trusted URLs. Otherwise parse the URL, allow-list scheme and host before the request, and disable or re-check redirects.

The label names a review area. Read each finding's evidence and assessment status to understand what scan established.
Finding-type glossary

Outbound Request Safety

Coverage: bounded analysis

Input used in an outbound request may affect its destination, headers, or other request values. Unrestricted destination control can lead to server-side request forgery (SSRF).

What this label tells you

The current scanner records bounded outbound-use evidence. A pill does not establish SSRF, destination reachability, or complete response-flow analysis.

Remediation approach

Verify allowed destinations and protocols, redirect handling, network boundaries, and sensitive request values for the specific HTTP client.

Finding-type glossary

Reflected XSS

Coverage: bounded server response and template analysis

Request-controlled content is reflected into a browser response in a context that allows it to execute as script.

What this label tells you

Request input reaches an HTML response or template output. Raw output is a construction finding; unknown context or sanitizers remain Review. Escaping protects only its modeled output context. Runtime exploitation is not implied.

Remediation approach

Use context-appropriate output encoding and safe template or DOM APIs. Input validation alone does not replace correct output handling.

The label names a review area. Read each finding's evidence and assessment status to understand what scan established.
Finding-type glossary

Stored XSS

Coverage: bounded model-field write/read and server template analysis

Untrusted data is persisted and later rendered in an active browser context.

What this label tells you

A matching model and field link source writes to rendering reads. Unresolved write origins remain Review; a verified raw path is a construction finding. A dependency version alone does not establish a reachable XSS flow.

Remediation approach

Preserve the intended stored data; apply context-appropriate escaping or maintained sanitization at every rendering sink, and review existing stored content.

Finding-type glossary

Sensitive Data Logging

Coverage: bounded analysis

Logs expose credentials, tokens, personal information, or other sensitive values to readers or storage that should not receive them.

What this label tells you

Covers verified console and winston calls in analyzed route handlers that receive request values with credential or personal-data evidence. A field name is a hint for review, not proof; Authorization and Cookie headers are credentials by HTTP semantics. Morgan request logging that records URLs is checked against credentials read from the query string. Constant messages are not leaks. Non-request secrets and logger redaction configuration are not yet analyzed.

Remediation approach

Avoid logging secrets; minimize sensitive fields and apply verified redaction before emission. Review log access and retention.

Finding-type glossary

Sensitive Operation Review

Coverage: bounded analysis

A possible sensitive use was recorded but does not yet map to a more specific supported risk family.

What this label tells you

This is an analysis category. Inspect the evidence and missing operation semantics before making a security claim.

Remediation approach

Resolve the source, operation, API behavior, and required protection before selecting a fix.
